# Identifier-preservation corpus: first measurement

Closes the gap flagged in `2026-09-25_score_12b.md` §4: chunk 8 promised a
corpus-level "OCR an image, assert the exact identifier string" harness, and
only two unit tests (`is_identifier` shape predicate, "lexicon holds nothing
the gate would suppress") existed. This is that harness's first run.

Labels: everything below is **measured**, from a real end-to-end pipeline run
(`Engine::recognize_lines`, real binarization/segmentation/decode — not the
oracle reader `pages::read_with_bank` uses elsewhere in this bench). Nothing
here is fitted or authored; the corpus content and the threshold proposal
are **authored by me** for this report and are explicitly not yet a gate.

## What was built

- `crates/ocrcer-bench/src/ident_corpus.rs` — authored identifier-shaped text
  blocks: `threads` (thread callouts, seeded with the known regression line
  `"4X M8x1.25 THRU"`), `parts` (part numbers, alloy codes), `revisions`,
  `dims` / `dims-ext` (dimensions, some using `Ø`/`±`/`°`), `accounts`
  (invoice/PO/RMA codes), `dates`. Each block mixes identifier-shaped tokens
  with plain words on the same line, matching real drawing-text density.
- `crates/ocrcer-bench/src/ident.rs` — classification logic. Calls
  `ocrcer_core::params::identifier_shape` (fed by
  `ocrcer_core::decode::viterbi::ClassInfo::of`) for identifier-shape
  classification, and `ocrcer_core::decode::lexicon::lookup` for the
  lexicon-rewrite sub-case. Neither predicate is reimplemented here.
- `crates/ocrcer-bench/src/bin/ident.rs` — `cargo run -p ocrcer-bench --bin
  ident generate <dir> <sizes>` and `... score <model> <dir> [--set k=v]...
  [--threshold N]`. Exits non-zero when `REWRITTEN > threshold`; **default
  threshold is 0** deliberately, so the binary never silently passes — a
  nonzero default would itself be "setting the gate to whatever passes."
- `bench/ident/` — generated corpus, gitignored (registered scoring-only via
  `.gitignore` comment + `bench/splits/README.md`, mirroring `bench/pages-cov/`;
  `bench/splits/manifest.tsv` is scoped to external-dataset row splits and
  does not apply to a purely-synthetic corpus).

Corpus: 53 licence-cleared faces (one face skipped — no defensible way to set
a line of text in it, same reason `pages-cov` excludes it) x 5 sizes
(14, 18, 21, 28, 40 px/em, matching `bench/pages-cov`'s own ladder, chosen to
avoid the documented bank-scale-collision artifact at 16/20/24/32/48) x 7
content blocks = 1855 pages. Every identifier-shaped ground-truth word on
every page is a test case: **6360 identifier-shaped tokens** total, identical
between the two runs below (`identifier_min_length`/`identifier_digit_fraction`
are not among the 11 fitted/control-divergent params, so shape classification
is the same in both).

**Corpus bias, stated plainly**: this corpus is deliberately identifier-dense
— far denser than a real drawing or invoice page — because the point is
exercising the predicate, not estimating identifier frequency in the wild.
Treat the counts below as "how the engine handles identifier-shaped text when
it sees a lot of it," not as a share of pdfcer's real corpus.

**Known predicate limitation, not a harness gap**: `identifier_shape` requires
`letters > 0`, so purely-numeric codes (e.g. an account number with no
letters) are never identifier-shaped under the authored predicate and are not
exercised by this test. That is a true statement about what the predicate
protects, not something this harness fails to cover.

## Measured results

Two runs against the same rebuilt model (`model/out/ocrcer.ocrw`, build
`d2997ef3`, matching `score_12b.md`'s build byte-for-byte):

| Config | Tested | Exact | Dropped/garbled | REWRITTEN | REWRITTEN rate |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **Fitted** (master defaults, no overrides) | 6360 | 3912 (61.51%) | 1177 (18.51%) | **1271** (19.98%) | — |
| **Control** (pre-fold 11-param `--set`, from `score_12b.md`) | 6360 | 3684 (57.93%) | 1207 (18.98%) | **1469** (23.10%) | — |

REWRITTEN split by kind:

| Config | to a different identifier | to a lexicon word |
|---|---:|---:|
| Fitted | 1268 | 3 |
| Control | 1459 | 10 |

**Reading this**: the fitted (post-fold) config has *fewer* REWRITTEN
identifiers than the control, in the same direction as the finfilings CER/WER
win reported in `score_12b.md` — the fold did not trade identifier safety for
prose accuracy on this corpus. But the CLAUDE.md rule-6 worry — the lexicon
silently turning an identifier into a dictionary word — is **not** the
dominant failure here: only 3 of 1271 (fitted) / 10 of 1469 (control)
REWRITTEN cases are lexicon-word rewrites. The other ~99% are identifier-to-
identifier corruption: the matcher/decoder reads a wrong but still
identifier-shaped string, which is just as invisible a failure under rule 6
even though it isn't the lexicon mechanism rule 6 was written about.

## Failure-mode breakdown (fitted config; control is the same shape, slightly worse)

Four identifiable, non-overlapping-by-construction clusters account for the
majority of the 1271 fitted REWRITTEN cases (counts are pattern matches
against the offender list, not a strict partition — a case can match more
than one heuristic):

1. **Multiplier case-drop, `NX` -> `Nx`** (`4X`->`4x`, `2X`->`2x`, `8X`->`8x`):
   **282 cases (22.2%)**. Present across nearly every face and every size,
   including 40px. This is the single largest cluster and it is narrow —
   one glyph pair, one confusion direction, no font or size dependency
   visible in the data.
2. **`0`/`O` digit-letter swap**, overwhelmingly `R0.5`->`RO.5` and siblings
   in the `dims` block: **121-125 cases (~9.7%)**. Concentrated in one
   content block (`dims`), present across most faces.
3. **Hyphen read as a different dash/math glyph** (`-` -> one of `– — ÷ · ~
   ° ≈ ‰`): **274 cases (21.6%)**, concentrated in `accounts` and `dates`
   blocks (`INV-2026-0042`, `PO-88213`, `L2026-0913-A`, etc.), spread across
   nearly all faces.
4. **Leading-token/leading-digit drop** (hypothesis is a suffix of the
   ground truth, e.g. `71-4820-B` -> `20-B` or `-4820-B`): **75 cases
   (5.9%)**, concentrated at the smallest sizes (14px) and at line starts.

Together these four clusters cover roughly 60% of the fitted REWRITTEN count
(756/1271); the remainder is a long tail of one-off substitutions with no
single dominant pattern.

## Translation for the other agents

- **`ocrcer-linguist`** — clusters 1 and 2 look like confusion-table /
  matcher-weight issues, not segmentation or font-coverage issues (they
  happen at every size including 40px, where segmentation is not under
  pressure). `X` vs `x` on an isolated multiplier callout and `0` vs `O`
  after `R` are both narrow, single-pair confusions with essentially no
  cross-font variance — that shape (one glyph pair, uniform across faces)
  is exactly what a confusion-table entry or a case-decision-boundary weight
  is for, not a font-bank fix. Cluster 3 (hyphen -> rare extended
  punctuation) looks like a class-frequency/prior problem: several rare
  math/typography glyphs (`÷ · ~ ° ≈ ‰`) are winning matches against a very
  common ASCII hyphen. Worth checking whether the confusion table or
  class-prior currently penalizes rare extended-charset glyphs enough when
  competing against common ASCII punctuation.
- **`ocrcer-runtime`** — cluster 4 (leading-token/leading-digit drop,
  concentrated at 14px and at token starts) looks like a segmentation
  problem, per `PLAN.md`'s risk-mitigation ladder (binarization/morphological
  repair before touching decoder weights) — small-size leading strokes are
  being lost rather than misread.
- **`ocrcer-glyphs`** — no cluster above points at missing font coverage;
  all four are present across nearly the whole face list rather than
  concentrated in one or two faces, which argues against "add more fonts to
  the bank" as the lever here. Worth a second opinion once linguist/runtime
  have had a pass, since a font-shape explanation can't be ruled out from
  this data alone, only deprioritized.
- **`ocrcer-architect`** — the corpus/harness format itself is new; if any of
  the above turns out to need a feature-vector or `.ocrw` format change
  rather than a weight/font/segmentation change, that decision is yours, not
  mine or the other agents'.

## Proposed threshold — NOT a decision, `ocrcer-architect` sets the gate

Measured baseline on current master: **1271 REWRITTEN (fitted)**, **1469
REWRITTEN (control)**, out of 6360 identifier-shaped tokens each (19.98% /
23.10%). A threshold of 0 — the binary's conservative default — fails both
configs today, as designed; it is not a gate that has ever passed.

I am not going to quietly pick a number that passes. Two honest options for
the architect to choose between, stated with their tradeoffs:

1. **Baseline-relative regression gate now** (e.g. threshold ~1300 fitted /
   ~1500 control, a few percent of headroom over today's measured count):
   catches regressions immediately, doesn't block chunk 8 on a pre-existing
   gap that predates this harness, but leaves the gate very loose relative
   to rule 6's stated bar ("worse than one that misreads it") for another
   ~20% of identifier-shaped tokens.
2. **Rate-based target tied to the failure-mode clusters above** (e.g. hold
   the line at today's count while linguist/runtime address clusters 1-4,
   then ratchet the threshold down each time a cluster closes, tracking
   `ident score`'s per-run count over time rather than fixing one number now):
   more honest about where the number should be headed, costs more process
   (a tracked ratchet instead of a static constant).

Either way, the number to gate on starts at the measured 1271 / 1469 above,
not at 0 and not at whatever the current build happens to produce after a
convenient rerun.
